%%% ===============================================================================
%                                  APPENDIX A
%%% ===============================================================================
\chapter{Hyperparameters and Configuration}
\label{app:hyperparameters}

This appendix lists the complete parameter sets of the \num{280} canonical runs.
\Zcref{sec:app-hp-baselines} gives the baseline parameters of each algorithm, \zcref{sec:app-hp-protocol} the settings of the measurement protocol that are common to all runs, and \zcref{sec:app-hp-sweeps} the override files that define the sweeps.
\Zcref{sec:app-hp-check} reports the check of the logged configurations against the baselines, and \zcref{sec:app-hp-refit} states how the refit interval of MBPO is evaluated.
The baseline settings and their deviations from the publications are discussed in \zcref{sec:baselines}.
All values were read from the configuration classes (\texttt{configs/\allowbreak config.py}) and checked against the \texttt{algo\_\allowbreak config} and \texttt{experiment\_\allowbreak config} fields of the \texttt{metadata.json} file of every canonical run.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Baseline Parameter Sets}\label{sec:app-hp-baselines}

\Zcref{tab:app-hp-sac}, \zcref{tab:app-hp-td3}, \zcref{tab:app-hp-mbpo} and \zcref{tab:app-hp-tdmpc2} list every field of the configuration class of one algorithm.
The baseline is the same on HalfCheetah-v5 and Ant-v5 except where a column for each environment is given.
Marked entries: $^{\dagger}$ implementation setting that is not taken from the publication; $^{\ddagger}$ deliberate deviation from the publication (\zcref{sec:baselines}).
Warmup is a protocol setting and is listed in \zcref{tab:app-hp-protocol}.

    \texttt{actor\_\allowbreak lr}, \texttt{critic\_\allowbreak lr}, \texttt{alpha\_\allowbreak lr}             & \num{3e-4}                    & Adam learning rates                          \\
    \texttt{gamma}                                                          & \num{0.99}                    & discount                                     \\

    \texttt{batch\_\allowbreak size}                                                    & \num{256}                     & minibatch size                               \\
    \texttt{buffer\_\allowbreak capacity}                                               & \num{1000000}                 & replay buffer, never filled at this run length \\
    \texttt{autotune\_\allowbreak alpha}                                                & true                          & automatic entropy tuning                     \\
    \texttt{init\_\allowbreak alpha}$^{\dagger}$                                        & \num{0.2}                     & initial temperature                          \\
    \texttt{target\_\allowbreak entropy}                                                & none ($-|A|$)                 & default heuristic, $|A|$ is the action dimension \\

    \texttt{policy\_\allowbreak update\_\allowbreak delay}                                          & \num{1}                       & actor updated on every update                \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Protocol Settings Common to All Runs}\label{sec:app-hp-protocol}

\Zcref{tab:app-hp-protocol} lists the settings of the experiment configuration (\texttt{ExperimentConfig}) that are identical in all \num{280} runs.
The measurement protocol itself is described in \zcref{sec:protocol}.
The clock lock, the persistence mode and the CPU governor are recorded as requested; no record shows that they took effect (\zcref{sec:data-quality}).

\begin{table}[tbp]
  \centering
  \footnotesize
  \caption{Protocol settings of the \num{280} canonical runs (\texttt{ExperimentConfig}).}
  \label{tab:app-hp-protocol}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}p{4.7cm}p{3.7cm}p{5.0cm}@{}}
    \toprule
    Setting                                                              & Value                          & Remark                                                   \\
    \midrule
    \texttt{train\_\allowbreak steps}                                                & \num{100000}                   & environment steps of the measured training              \\
    \texttt{steps\_\allowbreak per\_\allowbreak epoch}                                           & \num{1000}                     & one rollout and one gradient-update task per epoch      \\
    \texttt{warmup\_\allowbreak steps}                                               & \num{5000}                     & \num{10000} for TD3 (\num{80} runs); random actions, measured but excluded \\
    \texttt{settle\_\allowbreak seconds}                                             & \SI{30}{\second}               & unmeasured                                               \\
    \texttt{idle\_\allowbreak baseline\_\allowbreak seconds}, \texttt{idle\_\allowbreak tail\_\allowbreak seconds}       & \SI{90}{\second}, \SI{90}{\second} & idle windows before and after training               \\
    \texttt{measure\_\allowbreak power\_\allowbreak secs}                                        & \SI{1}{\second}                & CodeCarbon polling interval                             \\
    \texttt{tracking\_\allowbreak mode}                                              & \texttt{machine}               & whole-machine CPU, GPU and RAM terms                     \\
    \texttt{force\_\allowbreak cpu\_\allowbreak power\_\allowbreak w}                                        & none                           & no fixed CPU power (RAPL energy is read)                \\
    \texttt{lock\_\allowbreak gpu\_\allowbreak clocks}                                           & true                           & requested                                                \\
    \texttt{gpu\_\allowbreak min\_\allowbreak clock\_\allowbreak mhz}, \texttt{gpu\_\allowbreak max\_\allowbreak clock\_\allowbreak mhz}         & \SI{2000}{\mega\hertz}, \SI{2000}{\mega\hertz} & requested lock of the graphics clock               \\
    \texttt{set\_\allowbreak persistence\_\allowbreak mode}                                      & true                           & requested                                                \\
    \texttt{set\_\allowbreak cpu\_\allowbreak performance\_\allowbreak governor}                             & true                           & requested; governor \texttt{performance}                \\
    \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak enabled}                                      & true                           & wait between runs for the GPU to return to the reference state \\
    \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak temp\_\allowbreak tolerance\_\allowbreak c}                           & \SI{2}{\celsius}               & temperature tolerance                                    \\
    \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak power\_\allowbreak tolerance\_\allowbreak w}                          & \SI{5}{\watt}                  & power tolerance                                          \\
    \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak max\_\allowbreak wait\_\allowbreak seconds}                           & \SI{600}{\second}              & timeout of the gate                                      \\
    \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak poll\_\allowbreak interval\_\allowbreak seconds}                      & \SI{5}{\second}                & polling interval of the gate                             \\
    \texttt{device}                                                      & \texttt{cuda}                  & NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090                                  \\
    \texttt{country\_\allowbreak iso\_\allowbreak code}                                          & \texttt{DEU}                   & affects only the CO$_2$ conversion, which the thesis does not report \\
    Seeds                                                                & \num{331}, \num{958}, \num{14577}, \num{43611}, \num{85062} & in this order within every configuration          \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

The thermal gate reached its reference state in all \num{280} runs; none ran into the timeout (field \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate.reason} of the metadata).

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Sweep Override Files}\label{sec:app-hp-sweeps}

Hyperparameters other than the baseline values are set through JSON override files that are passed to the entry point with \texttt{--algo-config-overrides} (\zcref{sec:app-repro-run}).
Because only one file can be passed per run, the files of the Ant-v5 sweeps of MBPO and TD-MPC2 repeat the environment baseline (the rollout schedule of MBPO, \texttt{episodic} of TD-MPC2).
\Zcref{tab:app-hp-sweeps} lists the \num{37} files that the canonical runs use, with the parameter, the value and the number of canonical runs that were produced with the file.
The \num{6} baseline configurations without a file (SAC and TD3 on both environments, MBPO and TD-MPC2 on HalfCheetah-v5) use the defaults of \zcref{tab:app-hp-sac} to \zcref{tab:app-hp-tdmpc2}, and TD3 additionally the command-line option \texttt{--warmup-steps 10000}; they account for \num{30} runs, so the files account for \num{250} runs.
The runs of a file and the logged configuration of each run were matched by file name; for all \num{280} runs, the dataclass default with the file applied equals the \texttt{algo\_\allowbreak config} logged in the metadata (\zcref{sec:app-hp-check}).

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Check of the Logged Configurations}\label{sec:app-hp-check}

The \texttt{metadata.json} file of every canonical run stores the full algorithm configuration and the experiment configuration of the run.
The following checks were run on all \num{280} files.
\begin{itemize}
  \item The logged \texttt{algo\_\allowbreak config} equals the default of the configuration class of the algorithm with the override file of \zcref{tab:app-hp-sweeps} applied, for all \num{280} runs (no deviation).
  \item For each of the \num{56} configurations, the logged \texttt{algo\_\allowbreak config} differs from that of the baseline of the same algorithm and environment in exactly the swept parameter and in no other field (no exception).
        For TD3, the baseline has batch size \num{100}, so the batch-size sweep changes this one field.
  \item Within each configuration, the \texttt{algo\_\allowbreak config} and the \texttt{experiment\_\allowbreak config} are identical for the five seeds, apart from the seed itself and from one logging field described next.
  \item The \texttt{experiment\_\allowbreak config} is identical in all \num{280} runs except for \texttt{warmup\_\allowbreak steps} (\num{10000} in the \num{80} TD3 runs, \num{5000} otherwise).
\end{itemize}
The only exception is a logging field, not a setting: the field \texttt{thermal\_\allowbreak gate\_\allowbreak reference\_\allowbreak file} is stored in the metadata of only three runs (SAC on HalfCheetah-v5, baseline, seeds \num{331}, \num{958} and \num{14577}) and is absent from the other \num{277}, because the runner excludes it from the logged configuration from the fourth canonical run on (\zcref{sec:app-repro-code}).
The thermal gate was enabled and reached its reference state in all \num{280} runs.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Refit Interval of the MBPO Dynamics Model}\label{sec:app-hp-refit}

The configuration field \texttt{model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak freq} is \num{250}, and the training loop of MBPO (\texttt{algorithms/\allowbreak mbpo.py}, function \texttt{train}) refits the ensemble when
\begin{center}
  \footnotesize\texttt{(global\_\allowbreak step - last\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak step) >= model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak freq}
\end{center}
holds.
This condition is evaluated once at the start of every epoch of \num{1000} environment steps, and \texttt{last\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak step} is initialised to $-250$, so the first epoch also fits.
At every epoch the difference is \num{1000} and therefore at least \num{250}, so the model is refitted at the start of every epoch: \num{100} fits per run, one per \texttt{dynamics\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak update} task, and not one fit every \num{250} real steps.
The synthetic rollouts are generated once per epoch as well, directly after the rollout block, so the model buffer is refreshed once per epoch.
The logged number of fit epochs confirms this: in all \num{80} canonical MBPO runs, all \num{100} epochs of \texttt{training\_\allowbreak metrics.json} carry the diagnostics of a fit (\texttt{model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak epochs} is set), i.e.\ there are exactly \num{100} fits per run.
Each fit continues from the weights of the previous one and refits only the input normaliser; the fit stops early after \texttt{model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak patience} epochs without improvement of the holdout error.

\paragraph{Deviation from the publication.} The publication~\cite{janner2019mbpo} lists \num{20} policy updates per environment step on Ant and \num{40} on HalfCheetah, and \num{400} model rollouts per environment step.
The baselines of this thesis use one update per environment step, refit and refill once per epoch, and draw \num{10000} start states for each refill, i.e.\ \num{10} per environment step.
The reasons for this reduction and the resulting limits are given in \zcref{sec:baselines} and \zcref{sec:thr-design}.

%%% ===============================================================================
%                                  APPENDIX B
%%% ===============================================================================
\chapter{Full Result Tables}
\label{app:full-results}

This appendix gives the results of \zcref{ch:per-algorithm} and \zcref{ch:comparison} at full precision and in the full extent that the main text refers to.
Nothing is added to the interpretation.
\Zcref{sec:app-full-ch4} contains the tables of the four sections of \zcref{ch:per-algorithm} for all configurations, \zcref{sec:app-full-ec} the statistics behind \zcref{sec:energy-vs-compute}, \zcref{sec:app-full-rob} the orderings behind \zcref{sec:robustness}, and \zcref{sec:app-full-cross} the cross-seed table of all \num{56} configurations.
\Zcref{sec:app-full-opt} holds optional blocks that wait for a decision of the author.
All tables are generated from the raw run files and the context files of the repository by \texttt{flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak appendix\_\allowbreak tables.py}.

\paragraph{Conventions.} As in \zcref{ch:per-algorithm}, a value is the mean over the five canonical seeds and the number after the plus--minus sign is the sample standard deviation ($n-1$); means are given with six and spreads with four significant digits.
All energies are gross (\zcref{sec:units}).
J/FLOP is the ratio of the mean energy to the mean FLOPs, and its spread is the standard deviation of the five per-seed ratios.
Shares and percentages are computed per run and then averaged.
The segment names are abbreviated in the tables; the abbreviations are listed in each caption.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Tables of the Per-Algorithm Chapter}\label{sec:app-full-ch4}

Each subsection contains, for all configurations, the content of the tables of the corresponding section of \zcref{ch:per-algorithm}: energies and shares, FLOPs and energy per FLOP, durations, power and throughput, the composition of the measured energy with the idle-floor fractions, and the measurement diagnostics.
The tables of the baselines and of the sweeps of the main text are subsets of these tables.
The values of the main text agree with them to the rounding of the digits printed there (\zcref{tab:app-verification}).

    \caption[SAC: composition of the energy of the measured tasks by hardware term (CPU, GPU, RAM) and idle-floor fraction (mean $\pm$ sd of the per-run percentages over the five seeds)]{SAC: composition of the energy of the measured tasks by hardware term (CPU, GPU, RAM) and idle-floor fraction (mean $\pm$ sd of the per-run percentages over the five seeds). The RAM term is the constant capacity heuristic of CodeCarbon. The idle-floor fraction is a diagnostic: the mean of the head and tail idle power of the run (as recorded) times the duration of the task, divided by the gross energy of the task; it is not subtracted anywhere. Segment abbreviations: buffer = \texttt{buffer\_\allowbreak sample}, critic = \texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: world-model step), actor = \texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: policy-prior step), target = \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update}, GU = \texttt{gradient\_\allowbreak updates}, fit = \texttt{dynamics\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak update}, synth = \texttt{synthetic\_\allowbreak rollout\_\allowbreak generation}, pretrain = \texttt{world\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak pretrain}.     \caption[MBPO: duration $T$, mean power $\bar P$ and achieved throughput (mean $\pm$ sd over the five seeds)]{MBPO: duration $T$, mean power $\bar P$ and achieved throughput (mean $\pm$ sd over the five seeds). The duration of a measured segment is the sum of the CodeCarbon task durations, that of an allocated sub-segment the summed host time of its timers, and the throughput of a sub-segment is its FLOPs divided by this host time. Segment abbreviations: buffer = \texttt{buffer\_\allowbreak sample}, critic = \texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: world-model step), actor = \texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: policy-prior step), target = \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update}, GU = \texttt{gradient\_\allowbreak updates}, fit = \texttt{dynamics\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak update}, synth = \texttt{synthetic\_\allowbreak rollout\_\allowbreak generation}, pretrain = \texttt{world\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak pretrain}. Buffer, critic, actor and target are allocated from the GU energy by timed share; the other segments are measured. HC is HalfCheetah-v5, Ant is Ant-v5.}

    \caption[MBPO: composition of the energy of the measured tasks by hardware term (CPU, GPU, RAM) and idle-floor fraction (mean $\pm$ sd of the per-run percentages over the five seeds)]{MBPO: composition of the energy of the measured tasks by hardware term (CPU, GPU, RAM) and idle-floor fraction (mean $\pm$ sd of the per-run percentages over the five seeds). The RAM term is the constant capacity heuristic of CodeCarbon. The idle-floor fraction is a diagnostic: the mean of the head and tail idle power of the run (as recorded) times the duration of the task, divided by the gross energy of the task; it is not subtracted anywhere. Segment abbreviations: buffer = \texttt{buffer\_\allowbreak sample}, critic = \texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: world-model step), actor = \texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update} (TD-MPC2: policy-prior step), target = \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update}, GU = \texttt{gradient\_\allowbreak updates}, fit = \texttt{dynamics\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak update}, synth = \texttt{synthetic\_\allowbreak rollout\_\allowbreak generation}, pretrain = \texttt{world\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak pretrain}. Idle difference is $|P_{\mathrm{tail}}-P_{\mathrm{head}}|/P_{\mathrm{head}}$ (mean $\pm$ sd over seeds, as recorded). HC is HalfCheetah-v5, Ant is Ant-v5.}

\subsection{Agreement with the Main Text}\label{sec:app-full-verify}

\Zcref{tab:app-verification} reports the comparison of the tables of this appendix with the tables printed in \zcref{ch:per-algorithm} and \zcref{ch:comparison}.
For each table of the main text, every printed number was looked up among the recomputed values and compared at the number of digits printed with it; the numbers that are not tabulated in this appendix (ratios between the environments and similar derived columns) were not looked up and are counted as not found.
In addition, the cells of the \num{15} baseline tables of \zcref{ch:per-algorithm} (energies and shares, J/FLOP, durations and power, composition) were compared row by row with the recomputed value of the same environment, segment and quantity: \num{844} of \num{846} cells equal the recomputed value rounded to the printed digits, and the other two differ in the last printed digit (the standard deviation of the energy of \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update} of TD-MPC2 on HalfCheetah-v5, printed \num{109.8}, recomputed \num{109.7498}; the standard deviation of the RAM share in the gradient updates of TD-MPC2 on HalfCheetah-v5, printed \num{0.1613}, recomputed \num{0.16125}, a rounding tie).

The last column is the largest relative difference of a number found, over the numbers printed with at least four digits.}
  \label{tab:app-verification}

\section{Energy versus Compute}\label{sec:app-full-ec}

\Zcref{tab:app-fits-means} gives the fixed and marginal cost $E=a+bF$ of every segment along every sweep (\zcref{tab:ec-fits} gives the training total), \zcref{tab:app-fits-runs} the same fits on the individual runs, and \zcref{tab:app-pairs} the marginal cost between all adjacent sweep values (\zcref{tab:ec-delta} gives SAC and TD3).

\section{Optional Blocks}\label{sec:app-full-opt}

A block that is not wanted is deleted together with its marker lines; the main text is not changed by any of them.

%%% ===============================================================================
%                                  APPENDIX C
%%% ===============================================================================
\chapter{FLOP-Counting Details}
\label{app:flop-details}

This appendix gives the derivations and the numbers behind the FLOP accounting of \zcref{sec:flop-accounting}.
\Zcref{sec:app-flop-rules} states the counting rules and derives the per-call FLOPs of the learners of SAC, TD3 and MBPO.
\Zcref{sec:app-flop-mbpo} derives the FLOPs of the MBPO model fit and of the synthetic rollouts and shows that the formula reproduces the logged FLOPs of two baseline runs exactly.
\Zcref{sec:app-flop-tdmpc2} does the same for the three counted regions of TD-MPC2, including the termination classifier.
\Zcref{sec:app-flop-table} tabulates the per-call FLOPs of all architecture signatures, and \zcref{sec:app-flop-checks} reports the cross-checks and the consistency checks of the totals.
Counts are matmul FLOPs, a multiply--add counts as two FLOPs, and no other operation is counted (\zcref{sec:flop-principle}).
The per-call constants were counted with \texttt{FlopCounterMode} on the real network classes; the measurement device recorded in \texttt{flops\_\allowbreak per\_\allowbreak call.json} is the CPU, and the counts depend only on the tensor shapes.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Counting Rules and Per-Call FLOPs of the Learners}\label{sec:app-flop-rules}

\paragraph{Notation.} A stack of dense layers is a list $L=((n_0,n_1),\dots,(n_{k-1},n_k))$.
Applied to $R$ rows, its forward pass costs
\begin{equation}
  F(R;L) = \sum_{i=1}^{k} 2\,R\,n_{i-1}n_i ,
  \label{eq:app-fwd}
\end{equation}
and $F(R;L_{2:})$ denotes the same sum without the first layer.
$|S|$ and $|A|$ are the observation and action dimension, $B$ is the batch size, and $n(L)=\sum_i (n_{i-1}n_i+n_i)$ is the number of parameters of $L$.

\paragraph{Backward pass.} Autograd runs the backward pass only where a gradient is needed.
A layer with trainable parameters costs one further $2Rn_{i-1}n_i$ for the weight gradient, and each layer whose input needs a gradient costs another $2Rn_{i-1}n_i$ for the input gradient.
The input of the first layer needs no gradient when it is data (a state, or a state--action pair taken from the buffer), so its input gradient is not computed.
When the first-layer input is itself the output of a network that is being trained (the action fed to the critic in the actor update), the input gradient of the first layer is computed in full, with respect to the whole concatenated input.
When a network is frozen (the critics in the actor update), its backward pass computes input gradients only and no weight gradients.
Hence, for a stack that is trained and whose first-layer input is data, the backward pass costs $F(R;L)+F(R;L_{2:})$, for a stack that is trained and whose input needs a gradient $2F(R;L)$, and for a frozen stack $F(R;L)$.

\paragraph{Networks.} SAC has a Gaussian policy with a shared trunk $T=((|S|,h_1),(h_1,h_2))$ and two heads $H=((h_2,|A|))$ for the mean and the log standard deviation, and twin critics $Q=((|S|+|A|,h_1),(h_1,h_2),(h_2,1))$.
The policy forward pass for $R$ rows is $F_\pi(R)=F(R;T)+2F(R;H)$.
TD3 has a deterministic policy $P=((|S|,h_1),(h_1,h_2),(h_2,|A|))$ with $F_P(R)=F(R;P)$ and the same twin critics.
The hidden sizes $h_1=h_2$ are $1024$ at the baseline.

\paragraph{Rollout.} One call of \texttt{rollout} is a policy forward pass for one observation: $F_\pi(1)$ for SAC and MBPO, $F_P(1)$ for TD3.

\paragraph{Critic update.} The counted region contains the target action from the policy (no gradient), the forward passes of both target critics and of both online critics, and the backward pass of both online critics (weights; the first-layer input is data):
\begin{align}
  f_{\texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update}}^{\mathrm{SAC}} &= F_\pi(B) + 4F(B;Q) + 2\bigl[F(B;Q)+F(B;Q_{2:})\bigr] ,\label{eq:app-critic-sac}\\
  f_{\texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update}}^{\mathrm{TD3}} &= F_P(B) + 4F(B;Q) + 2\bigl[F(B;Q)+F(B;Q_{2:})\bigr] .\label{eq:app-critic-td3}
\end{align}

\paragraph{Actor update.} The region contains the policy forward pass, the forward passes of the critics with frozen parameters, the input-gradient passes through the critics, and the backward pass of the policy.
SAC evaluates two critics, TD3 one:
\begin{align}
  f_{\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update}}^{\mathrm{SAC}} &= F_\pi(B) + 2F(B;Q) + 2F(B;Q) + \bigl[4F(B;H)+F(B;T)+F(B;T_{2:})\bigr] ,\label{eq:app-actor-sac}\\
  f_{\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update}}^{\mathrm{TD3}} &= F_P(B) + F(B;Q) + F(B;Q) + \bigl[F(B;P)+F(B;P_{2:})\bigr] .\label{eq:app-actor-td3}
\end{align}
In the policy backward pass of SAC, each of the two heads has weight and input gradients ($2F(B;H)$ each), and the trunk has weight gradients and input gradients except for the first layer.

\paragraph{Polyak average.} The elementwise count of \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update} is two operations per parameter of every averaged network: $4\,n(Q)$ for SAC (both critics) and $2\,[\,n(P)+2n(Q)\,]$ for TD3 (policy and both critics).
The call count of \texttt{target\_\allowbreak update} is $N_{\mathrm{env}}U$ for SAC and MBPO and $\lceil N_{\mathrm{env}}U/d\rceil$ for TD3 with $d=2$, because TD3 updates the targets together with the actor (\zcref{eq:call-counts}).

\paragraph{Worked example.} For SAC on Ant-v5 at the baseline ($|S|=105$, $|A|=8$, $B=256$, $h_1=h_2=1024$), the terms of \zcref{eq:app-critic-sac} and \zcref{eq:app-actor-sac} are $F_\pi(B)=\num{600309760}$, $F(B;Q)=\num{596639744}$ and $F(B;Q)+F(B;Q_{2:})=\num{1134034944}$.
Therefore $f_{\texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update}}=\num{600309760}+4\cdot\num{596639744}+2\cdot\num{1134034944}=\num{5254938624}$.
The policy backward term is $4F(B;H)+F(B;T)+F(B;T_{2:})=\num{1145569280}$, so $f_{\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update}}=\num{600309760}+4\cdot\num{596639744}+\num{1145569280}=\num{4132438016}$.
Both equal the counted values.
The Polyak count is $4\,n(Q)=4\cdot\num{1167361}=\num{4669444}$ operations.

\paragraph{Reconciliation with the textbook estimate.} A textbook estimate of the critic update takes three times the forward cost of both critics, $3\cdot2F(B;Q)=\num{3579838464}$.
The counted region is larger by the target computation, $F_\pi(B)+2F(B;Q)=\num{1793589248}$, and smaller by the input gradient of the first layer of both critics, which autograd does not compute, $2\cdot2B(|S|+|A|)h_1=\num{118489088}$: $\num{3579838464}+\num{1793589248}-\num{118489088}=\num{5254938624}$.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{MBPO: Model Fit and Synthetic Rollouts}\label{sec:app-flop-mbpo}

\paragraph{Ensemble members.} A member has a trunk and two heads (mean and log variance),
\begin{equation*}
  T_M=\bigl((|S|+|A|,200),(200,200),(200,200),(200,200)\bigr),\qquad H_M=\bigl((200,|S|+1)\bigr),
\end{equation*}
so $m_f(R)=F(R;T_M)+2F(R;H_M)$.
The training step of one member at batch size $b$ costs
\begin{equation}
  m_{fb}(b) = m_f(b) + \bigl[F(b;T_M)+F(b;T_{M,2:})\bigr] + 2\cdot2F(b;H_M) ,
  \label{eq:app-member}
\end{equation}
and one forward pass of the whole ensemble for one input row costs $\varepsilon=E\,m_f(1)$ with $E=7$ members.
Matmul FLOPs are exactly linear in the number of rows, so the per-sample cost of the training step is $\mu=m_{fb}(b)/b$.
On HalfCheetah-v5, $\mu=\num{781600}$ and $\varepsilon=\num{1845200}$; on Ant-v5, $\mu=\num{1064800}$ and $\varepsilon=\num{2590000}$.

\paragraph{One call of \texttt{fit()}.} The routine is called once per epoch (\zcref{sec:app-hp-refit}) with the whole real buffer.
Let $n$ be the buffer size at the call.
The buffer holds the warmup transitions and the transitions of the preceding epochs, so $n=\min(w+k\cdot\num{1000},\,\num{e6})$ for the fit of epoch $k$ with $w$ warmup steps.
The routine first splits off the holdout set,
\begin{equation}
  n_h=\max\bigl(1,\lfloor \rho\,n\rfloor\bigr),\qquad n_t=n-n_h ,
  \label{eq:app-split}
\end{equation}
with the holdout ratio $\rho=0.2$.
Each member draws its own bootstrap sample of $n_t$ rows from the training split.
In every fit epoch, each member is trained on its sample in minibatches of $b=256$ rows, the last minibatch being smaller, and then the full ensemble is evaluated on the $n_h$ holdout rows (for the early-stopping criterion and the selection of the elites).
Because the FLOPs are linear in the rows, the last minibatch costs exactly its number of rows times $\mu$.
Let $e$ be the number of fit epochs that the call ran (it stops early after \num{5} epochs without improvement of the holdout error, and at most after \num{200}).
Then
\begin{align}
  F_{\mathrm{train}} &= e\,E\,n_t\,\mu , &
  F_{\mathrm{holdout}} &= e\,n_h\,\varepsilon , &
  c_{\mathrm{steps}} &= e\Bigl\lceil \frac{n_t}{b}\Bigr\rceil ,
  \label{eq:app-fit}
\end{align}
and the FLOPs of the call are $F_{\mathrm{train}}+F_{\mathrm{holdout}}$.
The optimiser steps of one fit epoch update all members together, so $c_{\mathrm{steps}}$ is the number of optimiser steps, which is the call count of \texttt{dynamics\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak update}.
For a run, \zcref{eq:app-fit} is summed over the \num{100} fits, with $e$ taken from the logged field \texttt{model\_\allowbreak train\_\allowbreak epochs} of each epoch in \texttt{training\_\allowbreak metrics.json}.
Neither the normaliser fit nor the optimiser arithmetic is counted.
The share of the holdout evaluation, $F_{\mathrm{holdout}}/(F_{\mathrm{train}}+F_{\mathrm{holdout}})$, is \SI{7.776}{\percent} on HalfCheetah-v5 and \SI{7.993}{\percent} on Ant-v5, the same in all \num{35} and \num{45} MBPO runs to the displayed digits, because $n_h/n_t$ is the same at every fit.

\paragraph{Synthetic rollouts.} In \texttt{synthetic\_\allowbreak rollout\_\allowbreak generation}, every synthetic transition requires one policy forward pass and one forward pass of all ensemble members (the member is chosen after all members were evaluated), so the cost per synthetic sample is $F_\pi(1)+\varepsilon$.
The call count is the number of synthetic samples, read from the logged field \texttt{synthetic\_\allowbreak transitions\_\allowbreak generated}.

\paragraph{Worked example.} \Zcref{tab:app-flop-mbpo-example} applies \zcref{eq:app-fit} to the baseline run with seed \num{331} of each environment.
The sum of the formula over the \num{100} fits reproduces the logged FLOPs, the logged number of optimiser steps and the logged FLOPs of the synthetic rollouts exactly, as integers.

\begin{table}[tbp]
  \centering
  \footnotesize

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{TD-MPC2: Counted Regions and Termination Classifier}\label{sec:app-flop-tdmpc2}

TD-MPC2 has an encoder, a latent dynamics model, a reward head, a termination head (only if \texttt{episodic} is true), a policy prior and $K$ Q-networks.
With MLPs of two hidden layers of $m=512$ units and a latent dimension $z=512$, their stacks of dense layers are
\begingroup\small
\begin{align*}
  E_\mathrm{n} &= \bigl((|S|,256),(256,512)\bigr), & D &= \bigl((z+|A|,m),(m,m),(m,z)\bigr), & R_\mathrm{w} &= \bigl((z+|A|,m),(m,m),(m,101)\bigr), \\
  T_\mathrm{c} &= \bigl((z,m),(m,m),(m,1)\bigr), & \pi &= \bigl((z,m),(m,m),(m,2|A|)\bigr), & Q &= \bigl((z+|A|,m),(m,m),(m,101)\bigr),
\end{align*}
\endgroup
where $Q$ is one of the $K$ Q-networks.
$H$ is the planning horizon, $S=512$ the number of candidate sequences, $P=24$ the number of sequences seeded by the policy prior, $I=6$ the number of MPPI iterations and $B=256$ the batch size.

\paragraph{Planning call (\texttt{rollout}).} One call encodes the observation, unrolls the $P$ policy-seeded sequences, and evaluates the $S$ candidates in each iteration:
\begingroup\small
\begin{equation}
  f_{\texttt{rollout}} = F(1;E_\mathrm{n}) + H\,F(P;\pi) + (H-1)F(P;D) + I\Bigl[H\bigl(F(S;R_\mathrm{w})+F(S;D)+\mathbb{1}_{\mathrm{ep}}F(S;T_\mathrm{c})\bigr)+F(S;\pi)+K\,F(S;Q)\Bigr] ,
  \label{eq:app-plan}
\end{equation}
\endgroup
where $\mathbb{1}_{\mathrm{ep}}$ is one if the termination classifier is active.

\paragraph{World-model step (\texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update}).} With $HB$ rows for the stacked time steps,
\begingroup\small
\begin{align}
  f_{\texttt{critic\_\allowbreak update}} &= F(HB;E_\mathrm{n}) + F(HB;\pi) + K\,F(HB;Q) && \text{targets, no gradient}\notag\\
  &\quad + F(B;E_\mathrm{n}) + H\,F(B;D) + K\,F(HB;Q) + F(HB;R_\mathrm{w}) + \mathbb{1}_{\mathrm{ep}}F(HB;T_\mathrm{c}) && \text{forward}\notag\\
  &\quad + \bigl[F(B;E_\mathrm{n})+F(B;E_{\mathrm{n},2:})\bigr] + 2H\,F(B;D) + 2K\,F(HB;Q) + 2F(HB;R_\mathrm{w}) + 2\,\mathbb{1}_{\mathrm{ep}}F(HB;T_\mathrm{c}) && \text{backward.}
  \label{eq:app-wm}
\end{align}
\endgroup

\paragraph{Policy-prior step (\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update}).} With $(H+1)B$ rows $G$ in the latent space, the Q-networks are frozen, so their backward pass computes input gradients only; the loss uses two randomly chosen networks, but the backward pass of every network is executed:
\begingroup\small
\begin{equation}
  f_{\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak update}} = F(G;\pi) + K\,F(G;Q) + K\,F(G;Q) + \bigl[F(G;\pi)+F(G;\pi_{2:})\bigr] .
  \label{eq:app-prior}
\end{equation}
\endgroup

\paragraph{Polyak average.} The elementwise count is $2K\,[\,n(Q)+4m\,]$ operations per update, where $n(Q)$ counts the dense layers and the term $4m$ is the weight and bias of the two layer normalisations of each Q-network.

\paragraph{Full update and warm-up burst.} The full update is the sum of the three regions; \texttt{world\_\allowbreak model\_\allowbreak pretrain} counts \texttt{warmup\_\allowbreak steps} full updates, i.e.\ \num{5000} calls.

\paragraph{Termination classifier.} In the Ant-v5 configurations, the classifier is evaluated in the planning call (term $I\,H\,F(S;T_\mathrm{c})$ of \zcref{eq:app-plan}) and trained in the world-model step (forward pass and backward pass, $3F(HB;T_\mathrm{c})$).
At the baseline ($H=3$) the classifier costs \num{9673113600} FLOPs per planning call, \SI{17.22}{\percent} of the call, and \num{2418278400} FLOPs per world-model step, \SI{8.685}{\percent} of the step.
Over a baseline run it accounts for $\num{100000}\cdot\num{9673113600}+(\num{100000}+\num{5000})\cdot\num{2418278400}=\num{1221230592000000}$ FLOPs (\num{1.221e15}, \SI{12.12}{\percent} of the run total of \num{1.008e16}), the second term counting the world-model steps of the warm-up burst as well.
The classifier values of all five Ant-v5 signatures are in \zcref{tab:app-flop-tdmpc2}; the five HalfCheetah-v5 signatures have no classifier.

\section{Cross-Checks and Consistency Checks}\label{sec:app-flop-checks}

\paragraph{Analytic versus counted FLOPs.} The script \texttt{flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak verify\_\allowbreak analytic\_\allowbreak flops.py} derives the constants of \zcref{tab:app-flop-learners} to \zcref{tab:app-flop-tdmpc2} from the layer dimensions with the formulas of this appendix and compares them with the counted values.
It needs neither a GPU nor PyTorch.
\Zcref{tab:app-flop-crosscheck} gives the result for the \num{42} canonical signatures: all \num{188} comparisons are exact.
\todo{AUTHOR: \texttt{verify\_\allowbreak analytic\_\allowbreak flops.py} is not committed. Commit it and cite the commit hash here.}

\paragraph{Linearity in the batch size.} The count of one member's training step at batch size \num{256} equals \num{256}/\num{100} times its count at batch size \num{100}, exactly: \num{200089600} against \num{78160000} on HalfCheetah-v5 and \num{272588800} against \num{106480000} on Ant-v5 (\texttt{verify\_\allowbreak mbpo\_\allowbreak fit\_\allowbreak flops.py}).

\paragraph{MBPO fit formula.} The script \texttt{verify\_\allowbreak mbpo\_\allowbreak fit\_\allowbreak flops.py} wraps \texttt{FlopCounterMode} around the unmodified fit routine and compares the count with \zcref{eq:app-fit}.
For buffer sizes of \num{5000}, \num{12345} and \num{30000} (three fit epochs each) the formula matches the counted FLOPs exactly on both environments (\zcref{tab:app-flop-fitcheck}).

\paragraph{TD-MPC2: regions against the unmodified update.} For each of the \num{10} signatures, the sum of the counted regions of the gradient update (world-model step, policy-prior step, Polyak average, whose matmul count is zero) equals the count of one call of the unmodified \texttt{update()} exactly (relative difference \num{0}).

\paragraph{Consistency of the totals.} The script \texttt{flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak check\_\allowbreak totals.py} recomputes the training totals of every run from the raw run files.
\Zcref{tab:app-flop-totals} reports its checks restricted to the \num{280} canonical runs (the script itself passes on all run directories).
The third check compares the sum of the four allocated sub-segments in \texttt{segment\_\allowbreak energy.json} with the sum of the \num{100} \texttt{gradient\_\allowbreak updates} rows of the per-task CodeCarbon file.
Its result depends on how the per-task file is parsed: with exact parsing of the decimal numbers (as in \texttt{check\_\allowbreak totals.py}) the maximum relative deviation is \num{2.2e-16}, whereas the default fast float parser of \texttt{pandas} gives \num{4.8e-13}, and with \texttt{float\_\allowbreak precision='round\_\allowbreak trip'} it is \num{3.6e-16}; the deviation is therefore at the level of the parsing precision and the summation order.

\chapter{Learning check details}
\label{app:learning}

This appendix documents the returns of the training runs, which the thesis uses as a sanity check only and never in a comparison (\zcref{sec:metric}).
\Zcref{sec:app-learn-def} defines the quantities, \zcref{sec:app-learn-curves} shows the return curves of all configurations, \zcref{sec:app-learn-hc} and \zcref{sec:app-learn-ant} give the learning check of HalfCheetah-v5 and Ant-v5, and \zcref{sec:app-learn-means} the means of all \num{56} configurations.
\Zcref{sec:app-learn-spearman} holds an optional extension of the rank-correlation analysis of \zcref{tab:spearman}, and \zcref{sec:app-learn-random} the placeholder for the return of a random policy.
All values were recomputed from the \texttt{training\_\allowbreak metrics.json} files of the \num{280} canonical runs.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Definitions}\label{sec:app-learn-def}

The returns are those of the training episodes, which run under the behaviour policy of the algorithm (a stochastic sample for SAC and MBPO, Gaussian action noise for TD3, noisy planning for TD-MPC2).
There are no separate evaluation episodes.

For a run with $n$ completed training episodes, the \emph{first \SI{10}{\percent}} and the \emph{last \SI{10}{\percent}} are its first and last $k=\max(1,\lfloor n/10\rfloor)$ training episodes.
The warm-up episodes (random actions) and the incomplete episode at the end of the run are not counted.
On HalfCheetah-v5, every episode ends after \num{1000} steps, so $n=\num{100}$ and $k=\num{10}$ in all \num{135} runs.
On Ant-v5, episodes end early when the robot leaves its healthy state; $n$ lies between \num{154} and \num{2556} per run, and $k$ between \num{15} and \num{255}.
The \emph{return} of the first (last) tenth of a run is the unweighted mean of the returns of its $k$ episodes.
The \emph{return per step} is the sum of the returns divided by the sum of the lengths of the same episodes, i.e.\ a ratio of sums and not a mean of per-episode ratios.
Values of a configuration are means over the five seeds, and the spread is the sample standard deviation ($n-1$) over the seeds.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Return Curves}\label{sec:app-learn-curves}

\Zcref{fig:app-return-sac-hc} to \zcref{fig:app-return-tdmpc2-ant} show the return of the training episodes against the number of training steps, one figure per algorithm and environment.
The \num{100000} training steps are divided into ten windows of \num{10000} steps; a point is the mean return of the episodes that ended within the window, and the bars are the sample standard deviation over the five seeds.
The warm-up is not shown.
Each panel varies one parameter around the baseline; the baseline (black) is the same data in every panel of a figure.
The returns are those of the training policy.

\section{HalfCheetah-v5}\label{sec:app-learn-hc}

\Zcref{tab:app-learning-hc} lists the \num{27} HalfCheetah-v5 configurations.
In all \num{27} configurations the mean return of the last tenth is above that of the first tenth, and this holds in each of the five seeds of every configuration (\num{135} of \num{135} runs).
The mean return of the last tenth lies between \num{2921} (SAC, batch size \num{1024}) and \num{7756} (TD-MPC2, \texttt{num\_\allowbreak q} \num{7}).

In \num{13} of the \num{14} configurations the episodes of the last tenth are longer than those of the first tenth; only TD3 with batch size \num{1024} has shorter ones (\num{318} to \num{194} steps).
For MBPO the episodes become much longer (e.g.\ \num{235} to \num{958} steps for the baseline), and the return per step rises in seven of the nine configurations (baseline: from $-0.48$ to $-0.34$) and falls in two (rollout length \num{1} and width \num{256}); in most of them the episode return therefore falls although the return per step rises.
For SAC and TD3 on Ant-v5, late episodes are mostly terminated early (fraction of terminated late episodes \num{0.67} to \num{0.88} in the five flagged configurations).

\Zcref{tab:app-learning-perstep} adds the return per step of the \num{14} configurations (definition in \zcref{sec:app-learn-def}).
The return per step rises in \num{10} of the \num{14} configurations and does not rise in the four that are marked with an asterisk in \zcref{tab:app-learning}: MBPO with maximum rollout length \num{1}, MBPO with width \num{256}, SAC with batch size \num{1024} and TD3 with batch size \num{1024}.

\section{Rank Correlation of Return and Energy with FLOPs}\label{sec:app-learn-spearman}

% --- OPTIONAL BLOCK (Spearman analysis per environment and per algorithm): begin ---

\Zcref{tab:app-spearman} extends \zcref{tab:spearman} by the correlations within each algorithm.
The pooled rows reproduce \zcref{tab:spearman}.
The per-algorithm rows use the configurations of one algorithm in one environment ($n=5$ to $9$), which are not independent samples; the $p$-values are those of the $t$ approximation of \texttt{scipy.stats.spearmanr} and are descriptive.
For $|\rho|=1$ the approximation is not defined and no value is given.

\chapter{Reproducibility details}
\label{app:reproducibility}

This appendix documents what is needed to trace and repeat the experiments.
\Zcref{sec:app-repro-env} lists the software and hardware of the canonical runs, \zcref{sec:app-repro-sessions} the ten execution sessions, and \zcref{sec:app-repro-notes} two irregularities of the execution that did not change the data.
\Zcref{sec:app-repro-code} identifies the code state of the runs, \zcref{sec:app-repro-scripts} the scripts that produced the numbers of the thesis, and \zcref{sec:app-repro-run} how the experiments are started.
\Zcref{sec:app-repro-repo} states the open points of the repository.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Execution Sessions}\label{sec:app-repro-sessions}

The \num{280} canonical runs were launched in ten sessions (\zcref{tab:sessions}) from \num{12} git commits (\zcref{sec:app-repro-code}).
A session is a maximal sequence of runs in which the next run starts less than \SI{30}{\minute} after the end of the previous one.
The largest gap between two runs inside a session is \SI{9.6}{\minute}, and the smallest gap between two sessions is \SI{3.3}{\hour}, so every threshold between \SI{10}{\minute} and \SI{3}{\hour} gives the same ten sessions (checked for \SI{30}{\minute}, \SI{1}{\hour} and \SI{2}{\hour}).
The \num{280} runs last \SI{117.7}{\hour} in total (sum of the run durations) and the ten session spans add up to \SI{118.6}{\hour}.

\section{Notes on the Execution}\label{sec:app-repro-notes}

\paragraph{False start of the TD-MPC2 sweep.} On 18 September 2026 the script of the \texttt{num\_\allowbreak q}/horizon sweep was started with an override file that did not exist (\texttt{configs/\allowbreak overrides/\allowbreak tdmpc2\_\allowbreak num\_\allowbreak q3.json}; the files are named \texttt{tdmpc2\_\allowbreak numq3.json}).
The first five launches (HalfCheetah-v5, seeds \num{331}, \num{958}, \num{14577}, \num{43611} and \num{85062}, \texttt{num\_\allowbreak q} $=3$) each ended after about one second, between 12:18:39 and 12:18:42 local time, with a \texttt{FileNotFoundError} raised while the configuration was built, before the experiment runner, the clock control, the thermal gate or the tracker was started.
The sixth launch (Ant-v5, seed \num{331}, \texttt{num\_\allowbreak q} $=3$) has a start line and no further output; the script was stopped during it.
The sweep was relaunched at 12:21:00 local time, and all \num{40} runs analysed come from the relaunch.
None of the six launches created a run directory, so nothing was excluded; the first run directory of the sweep is the one of the relaunch (\texttt{20260918\_\allowbreak 122100}).
Whether the sixth launch changed the clock lock cannot be read from the log, because the messages of the control module are not logged.
The run header, which the runner writes before it acquires the clock lock, is missing for this launch, so the process had presumably not reached that point (inferred from the order of the code), and every later run sets the lock itself at its start.
The repository documentation mentions only the five failed launches.

\paragraph{Status files of the UTD sweep.} The script \texttt{scripts/\allowbreak run\_\allowbreak utd\_\allowbreak sweep.sh} was first used for the UTD sweeps of SAC (Ant-v5) and TD3 and was then reused, with different settings, for the UTD sweep of MBPO.
Its status file \texttt{results/\allowbreak \_\allowbreak utd\_\allowbreak sweep\_\allowbreak status.json} therefore describes only the MBPO sweep (\num{20} runs, all done).
The log \texttt{results/\allowbreak \_\allowbreak utd\_\allowbreak sweep.log} was appended to, not overwritten, and holds \num{50} launch blocks: SAC on Ant-v5 (\num{10}), TD3 on both environments (\num{20}), which end with the completion line ``\num{30}/\num{30} runs succeeded'', and MBPO (\num{20}, ``\num{20}/\num{20} runs succeeded'').
Every block of the first \num{30} has a run directory.
The SAC UTD runs on HalfCheetah-v5 have their own log (\texttt{results/\allowbreak sac/\allowbreak HalfCheetah-v5/\allowbreak \_\allowbreak utd\_\allowbreak sweep.log}, \num{10} blocks, ``\num{10}/\num{10} runs succeeded'').
All \num{12} UTD configurations have five run directories.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Code Provenance}\label{sec:app-repro-code}

The commit stored in the metadata of a run is the \texttt{HEAD} at its start; uncommitted changes are not captured, and no flag marks a dirty working tree.
The \num{280} runs record \num{12} commits (\zcref{tab:app-repro-commits}).
Between them, the training and measurement code (\texttt{algorithms/\allowbreak }, \texttt{configs/\allowbreak config.py}, \texttt{experiment\_\allowbreak runner.py}, \texttt{run\_\allowbreak experiment.py}, \texttt{utils/\allowbreak }) changes in three ways only: the TD3 and TD-MPC2 modules are added together with their configuration classes and \num{8} lines of dispatch in the runner; the start and end temperature and power were added to the metadata and the thermal-gate file name was removed from the logged configuration (logging only); and the default hidden size of MBPO was set to \num{1024} before the MBPO baselines, all of which record \num{1024}.
The SAC and MBPO implementations, the dynamics model, the buffers and the clock-control utilities are identical at all \num{12} commits (checked with \texttt{git diff} between the first and the last recorded commit).

    The last column lists the changes of the training and measurement code relative to the previous row.}
  \label{tab:app-repro-commits}
  \resizebox{\textwidth}{!}{%
    \begin{tabular}{@{}llp{4.6cm}rp{3.2cm}p{5.6cm}@{}}
      \toprule
      Commit & Date (UTC+2) & Subject & Runs & Algorithm, environment & Change of the code relative to the previous row \\
      \midrule
      \texttt{f23f448} & 2026-09-07 12:13 & Setting baseline config for all experiments & \num{8} & SAC HC 5, Ant 3 & first recorded commit \\
      \texttt{92dcee0} & 2026-09-07 14:13 & Recorded runs for seeds - 331, 958, 14577, 43611, 85062 & \num{2} & SAC Ant 2 & runner and thermal gate: start and end temperature and power added to the metadata; thermal-gate file name no longer written to the logged configuration (logging only) \\
      \texttt{ecb98bb} & 2026-09-08 12:20 & Finished remaining baseline runs for Ant/v5 & \num{10} & SAC HC 10 & none \\
      \texttt{0bbbbb7} & 2026-09-08 19:44 & baseline config for mbpo & \num{10} & MBPO HC 5, Ant 5 & default hidden size of MBPO $(256,256)\to(1024,1024)$ (one line of the configuration) \\
      \texttt{58962d2} & 2026-09-09 12:15 & Added and tested td3 algorithm & \num{10} & TD3 HC 5, Ant 5 & TD3 module (\texttt{algorithms/\allowbreak td3.py}), \texttt{TD3Config}, four lines of dispatch in the runner \\
      \texttt{aa9186b} & 2026-09-09 13:42 & Setup UTD sweep run script for SAC and TD3 across both envs and 5 seeds & \num{30} & SAC Ant 10; TD3 HC 10, Ant 10 & none \\
      \texttt{b5b7e54} & 2026-09-10 12:27 & Setup new  algorithm tdmpc2 and tested recording & \num{10} & TD-MPC2 HC 5, Ant 5 & TD-MPC2 module (\texttt{algorithms/\allowbreak tdmpc2.py}), \texttt{TDMPC2Config}, four lines of dispatch in the runner \\
      \texttt{7e672ef} & 2026-09-12 19:14 & Setting up width sweep for SAC, TD3 and MBPO & \num{60} & SAC HC 10, Ant 10; TD3 HC 10, Ant 10; MBPO HC 10, Ant 10 & none \\
      \texttt{ecb7135} & 2026-09-14 12:48 & Scheduling UTD sweep for MBPO & \num{20} & MBPO HC 10, Ant 10 & none \\
      \texttt{9b4b44c} & 2026-09-15 12:36 & Preparing batch size sweep for Half Cheetah & \num{35} & SAC HC 10; TD3 HC 15; MBPO HC 10 & none \\
      \texttt{788ffcd} & 2026-09-17 12:10 & Scheduling batch sweep for ant and new rollout sweep for MBPO & \num{45} & SAC Ant 10; TD3 Ant 15; MBPO Ant 20 & none \\

      \bottomrule
    \end{tabular}}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Working tree of the first group of SAC runs.} The exception to the rule that the recorded commit describes the code is the first group of SAC baseline runs.
The eight runs that record \texttt{f23f448} differ in the fields of the thermal-gate entry of their metadata: the first three have neither the thermal reference nor the start and end values, the fourth has the thermal reference fields (\texttt{reference\_\allowbreak temp\_\allowbreak c}, \texttt{reference\_\allowbreak power\_\allowbreak w}), and the fifth to eighth have the start and end temperature and power.
The first three runs also record the name of the thermal reference file in the experiment configuration, which the later runs do not.
The working tree was therefore edited between the third and fourth and between the fourth and fifth run; as far as the difference to the next recorded commit (\texttt{92dcee0}) shows, the edits concern logging only.

\paragraph{Override files.} The override files \texttt{sac\_\allowbreak utd2.json} and \texttt{sac\_\allowbreak utd4.json} first appear in the repository in \texttt{0bbbbb7}, after the ten SAC UTD runs on HalfCheetah-v5 that record \texttt{ecb98bb}; the configuration logged by these runs equals the default with the files applied (\zcref{sec:app-hp-check}).

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{How to Run}\label{sec:app-repro-run}

\paragraph{Environment.} The experiments run on Linux in a plain virtual environment (\texttt{rl-exp}, no conda) created from \texttt{requirements.txt}.
The clock lock, the persistence mode and the CPU governor need root, so each run is started with \texttt{sudo} and the interpreter of the environment, for example
\begin{center}
  \texttt{sudo rl-exp/\allowbreak bin/\allowbreak python run\_\allowbreak experiment.py --algo sac --env HalfCheetah-v5 --seed 331}
\end{center}
Without root, these controls fail with a warning and the run proceeds unlocked.
Hyperparameters are not set by command-line flags but by the configuration classes; deviations are passed as a JSON override file (\texttt{--algo-config-overrides}, \zcref{sec:app-hp-sweeps}).

\Zcref{tab:app-repro-cli} lists the options of the entry point.
All baselines use the defaults except TD3, which is started with \texttt{--warmup-steps 10000}.

\begin{table}[tbp]
  \centering
  \footnotesize
  \caption{Options of \texttt{run\_\allowbreak experiment.py}.}
  \label{tab:app-repro-cli}
  \begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
    \toprule
    Option                                                          & Default            & Meaning                                                 \\
    \midrule
    \texttt{--algo}                                                 & \texttt{sac}       & \texttt{sac}, \texttt{td3}, \texttt{mbpo} or \texttt{tdmpc2} \\
    \texttt{--env}                                                  & \texttt{HalfCheetah-v5} & Gymnasium environment                              \\
    \texttt{--seed}                                                 & \num{0}            & seed of the run                                         \\
    \texttt{--device}                                               & \texttt{cuda}      & device (falls back to the CPU)                          \\
    \texttt{--train-steps}, \texttt{--steps-per-epoch}              & \num{100000}, \num{1000} & measured environment steps, steps per epoch       \\
    \texttt{--warmup-steps}                                         & \num{5000}         & random-action steps before training                     \\
    \texttt{--settle-seconds}                                       & \num{30}           & unmeasured settle time                                  \\
    \texttt{--idle-baseline-seconds}, \texttt{--idle-tail-seconds}  & \num{90}, \num{90} & idle windows                                            \\
    \texttt{--no-gpu-lock}, \texttt{--no-thermal-gate}              & off                & disable the clock lock, the thermal gate                \\
    \texttt{--tracking-mode}                                        & \texttt{machine}   & CodeCarbon tracking mode                                \\
    \texttt{--output-dir}                                           & \texttt{results}   & output directory                                        \\
    \texttt{--country-iso-code}                                     & \texttt{DEU}       & grid region (CO$_2$ conversion only)                    \\
    \texttt{--algo-config-overrides}                                & none               & JSON file with hyperparameter overrides                 \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Thermal gate.} At the start of each process, the runner captures a stable reading of the GPU temperature and power as the reference of that process, polls every \SI{5}{\second} until both are within \SI{2}{\celsius} and \SI{5}{\watt} of it, for at most \SI{600}{\second} (\zcref{tab:app-hp-protocol}), and then settles for \SI{30}{\second} before the idle window of the head.

\paragraph{Output of a run.} A run writes six files to \texttt{results/\{algo\}/\{env\}/seed\_\allowbreak \{seed\}/\{timestamp\}/}: \texttt{metadata.json} (commit, versions, GPU, full configuration, thermal-gate information), \texttt{segment\_\allowbreak energy.json} (energy per segment in kWh and the timer totals of the allocated sub-segments), \texttt{training\_\allowbreak metrics.json} (episodes and per-epoch diagnostics), \texttt{emissions.csv} (CodeCarbon whole-run row), \texttt{emissions\_\allowbreak base\_\allowbreak \{id\}.csv} (CodeCarbon task rows: one row per task, i.e.\ the idle windows, the warm-up and the rollout and gradient-update task of every epoch) and \texttt{run.log}.

\paragraph{Sweep scripts.} The runs were started by shell scripts in \texttt{scripts/\allowbreak } (\zcref{tab:app-repro-sweeps}).
Each script loops over the configurations and the five seeds, writes a status file in JSON and a log, primes the \texttt{sudo} credential once at the start and refreshes it in the background every \SI{60}{\second} for the life of the sweep, and, except for the three baseline scripts, powers the machine off after the last run.
The ten SAC baseline runs were started by hand and have neither script nor status file.

\paragraph{Analysis.} The FLOP pipeline is run from the repository root: \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak measure\_\allowbreak flops.py} (per-call FLOPs, needs PyTorch), \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak compute\_\allowbreak energy\_\allowbreak per\_\allowbreak flop.py} (per-run and cross-seed tables, canonical seeds by default), \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak check\_\allowbreak totals.py} and \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak verify\_\allowbreak analytic\_\allowbreak flops.py} (checks of \zcref{app:flop-details}).
The correlation analysis is \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak correlation\_\allowbreak analysis.py}, and the tables of \zcref{app:full-results} are written by \texttt{python flop\_\allowbreak analysis/\allowbreak appendix\_\allowbreak tables.py}.

\chapter{Clock Experiment Details}
\label{app:clock-details}

This appendix documents the two runs of \zcref{ch:clock}.
\Zcref{app:clock-provenance} lists what the repository shows about their origin, \zcref{app:clock-suballoc} gives the energy of the sub-segments allocated within the gradient updates, \zcref{app:clock-idle} the idle windows, and \zcref{app:clock-full} all segments of both runs.
R2000 is the run \texttt{results/\allowbreak mbpo/\allowbreak HalfCheetah-v5/\allowbreak seed\_\allowbreak 0/\allowbreak 20260904\_\allowbreak 122958} and R200 the run \texttt{results/\allowbreak mbpo/\allowbreak HalfCheetah-v5/\allowbreak seed\_\allowbreak 0/\allowbreak 20260904\_\allowbreak 130915}.
All values were recomputed from the run files (\texttt{segment\_\allowbreak energy.json} and the per-task CodeCarbon file).

\section{Provenance of the Two Runs}
\label{app:clock-provenance}

\begin{itemize}
  \item Both runs record the commit \texttt{ae38808}, which dates from 2026-09-02 (14:53 UTC+2) and predates the MBPO implementation (first committed in \texttt{f109bd4} on 2026-09-04, which also contains both run directories). Only \texttt{git rev-parse HEAD} is stored, so both runs executed uncommitted working-tree code.
  \item Both runs record the same software (PyTorch 2.13.0+cu130, CodeCarbon 3.3.0), the hidden sizes $(256,256)$ and the clock request of their column (\num{2000}/\num{2000}\,MHz and \num{200}/\num{200}\,MHz); \texttt{lock\_\allowbreak gpu\_\allowbreak clocks} is true in both.
  \item At \texttt{ae38808} and \texttt{f109bd4} the committed default of the two clock fields is \texttt{None}, whereas both runs record explicit values. The explicit values therefore come from a working-tree edit; its form is not recorded. No command-line flag for the clock values exists.
  \item Later commits set the default to $2000/2000$\,MHz (commit \texttt{f23f448}, 2026-09-07); all canonical and sweep runs record this value.
  \item Not found in the repository: reason for trying \SI{200}{\mega\hertz}, exact command line, GPU power limit, supported and maximum clocks, driver and CUDA versions, and temperatures before and after the idle windows.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Unset clock fields in development runs.}
Of the \num{300} run directories in the repository, \num{280} are the canonical runs; the other \num{20} are development runs.
Fifteen of them, all SAC runs of 13 August to 2 September 2026, record \texttt{None} for both clock fields; the other five (the two runs of this chapter and one development run each of MBPO, TD3 and TD-MPC2) record \num{2000}/\num{2000}\,MHz or, for R200, \num{200}/\num{200}\,MHz.
The fifteen runs record the commits \texttt{97eb7a7}, \texttt{64fb0cd}, \texttt{20b4d4f}, \texttt{4b9f11a}, \texttt{2aa4ccd}, \texttt{cf4d327}, \texttt{d05cbe6} and \texttt{d5880ed}.
In the source of all eight commits, and of \texttt{ae38808}, an unset field means that the clock-control function queries the supported graphics clocks with \texttt{nvidia-smi} and sets any unset bound to the \emph{lowest} supported clock (comment in the code: ``lock to a single fixed low clock by default''); the value of that clock was not recorded.
The constant \texttt{DEFAULT\_\allowbreak LOCK\_\allowbreak MHZ} first appears in \texttt{f109bd4} (2026-09-04) with the value \num{200}, applied to unset bounds after clamping to the supported range, and has the value \num{2000} from \texttt{f23f448} (2026-09-07), together with the configuration defaults of \num{2000}.
The repository documentation (\texttt{README.md} and \texttt{CLAUDE.md}) describes the fifteen runs as equivalent to \SI{2000}{\mega\hertz}; the commit history shows no code that gives an unset field the value \num{2000} before \texttt{f23f448}.
\todo{AUTHOR: decide how the unset clock fields of the fifteen development runs are described (they are not part of the 280 canonical runs), and correct the statement in \texttt{README.md} and \texttt{CLAUDE.md}.}

\paragraph{Allocated power.}
The allocated powers (\SI{113.6}{\watt} and \SI{156.0}{\watt}) are energy divided by the timed sub-segment duration and are not comparable to the measured power of gradient updates (\SI{110.9}{\watt} and \SI{95.8}{\watt}).
All four sub-segments of a run share the same allocated power, because the allocation splits the measured energy in proportion to the timed durations (\zcref{eq:allocation}).
